One dated entry per step. Times are the author's local time (America/New_York).

- 2026-10-26 11:44 to 12:2x: Repository created. - 2026-10-27 03:2x to 04:0x: **Step 4 started: the tokenizer (`src/tok/`).** Byte-level BPE read from the GGUF vocabulary (`tokenizer.ggml.*`, model gpt2, pre-tokenizer qwen35): added tokens matched first (leftmost-longest; control tokens only with parse-special), NFC (tables generated from Python 3.13 unicodedata, Unicode 15.1, by `tools/gen_unicode_tables.py`), the qwen35 split implemented alternative by alternative from the pattern's own semantics, merges by rank with ties to the leftmost pair. `io/gguf_reader` gained one-pass array readers. CLI: `pelias tokenize`. Unit tests 35/35 on gcc, clang and gcc-asan. **P-D1 against llama.cpp (2ca15f5 tokenizer code, llama-tokenize built CPU-only in the sandbox, `--no-escape`, Pelias with NFC off as llama.cpp does not normalise): 0 mismatches over 7 files, 2,455,727 tokens** (code, markdown, the model card and template, 1 MB of fuzz over every assigned code point, combining marks, added tokens inside text; plus unassigned code points: identical). Pelias's NFC equals Python's NFC on all 7 files. End-to-end process time on the same files, sandbox (2 cores): llama-tokenize 12.28 s, `pelias tokenize` 2.83 s (MEASURED, median of 3). - 2026-10-27 09:4x to 10:3x: **P-D1 against Hugging Face, first run** (author's machine, transformers 4.57.6, tokenizers 0.22.2): 4 of 7 files identical; 3 mismatched (fuzz_assigned, marks, specials), each at a combining-mark reordering. `tools/hf_unicode_probe.py` found why: the reference's split pattern (Oniguruma) classifies by **Unicode 16.0** (4,302 letters, 51 marks, 80 numbers new since 15.1), while its NFC (crate unicode-normalization-alignments 0.1.12, READ-SRC: `UNICODE_VERSION = (9, 0, 0)`) uses **Unicode 9.0** data, so marks added after 9.0 have combining class 0 there. Pelias's tables were Python 3.13 / Unicode 15.1 for both. Fix: `tools/gen_unicode_tables.py` takes categories from Python 3.14 (Unicode 16.0) and NFC data from the crate's own `src/tables.rs` (`--nfc-from-crate`); the probe now compares the library's NFC with Pelias's NFC over the same tables (its Python re-expression checked: 0 differences against Python's NFC on the old 15.1 tables, 65,666 sequences). Unit tests 35/35; llama.cpp parity (NFC off) unchanged on all 7 files. On characters new in Unicode 16.0 llama.cpp's own tables split differently (its table version is not 16.0, UNVERIFIED which): the Hugging Face tokenizer is the reference for training (author, 2026-10-27).

Each 256-segment is dequantized with the reference's own float expressions (every w bit-identical to quant::dequantize_row), planes added in the reference's order, products summed with FMA into 32 lanes and a fixed tree. AVX2 + FMA dequantizers for Q8_0, Q4_K, Q5_K, Q6_K, IQ4_XS, Q4_TG, Q4_TG2 (16-level float table by two permutes + blend); every other readable type through dequantize_row per segment; run-time dispatch (target attributes, MSVC cpuid). `ForwardOptions::fast_cpu` / `--fast-cpu` routes every matmul row through it; `pelias bench-dot` measures GB/s per type. - 2026-10-31 03:2x-03:5x: **GP3-14 doorbell + MTP live bank (author 03:18; sandbox, 4c736fc).** Doorbell as designed: pinned mapped flags; per layer the GPU copies the router's picks and normed rows to pinned memory and rings; a CPU thread runs that layer's cold picks into pinned memory and rings back; the GPU waits in-stream (__nanosleep 64 -> 200 ns) and reads the cold sum from host memory. A step is enqueued with no host wait (one sync for the logits); cancel / 30 s stall paths ring zeros so no GPU wait hangs. MTP bank: picks counted per expert on every MTP step; fixed arena reserved at open (the trunk's per-layer average share, DERIVED at open); mtp_promote refills it with the most picked experts (whole bank re-uploaded when the set changes: about 1.9 MB per expert, Q4_K gate / up + Q5_K down on the tiered file's blk.40; upload only the swapped experts later); promoted after the prompt and every 32 drafting steps. rung4: doorbell vs syncs bit for bit. Open next: the CUDA Graph per step (U8), wiring R3 (kern/gpu_stream, the measured 5.685 ms decode kernel) into M = 1 steps, DeltaNet prefill path after the first runs (author).

- 2026-10-31 20:4x-21:xx: **v26 code (author 20:39: "Don't hold back on building any part of it. build it and sandbox it and run it."; the plan of 20:28-20:35 after the v25 trace).** Each part behind an engine-check switch. (1) Reader v3 (moe_reader3, default with the reader; --reader-v25 for the old): the Q4_TG2 grid replicated per lane in shared memory (R3's grid path: decode32's floats, conflict-free), two rows staged per warp, the down rows by half-warps when F / 32 <= 16 (F = 512: v25 left half of each warp idle); each pick's arithmetic is v25's (bit for bit). f32 rows (the routers) read as float4 in k_matmul (same floats and order). (2) Reader cache (--reader-cache K): K slots per layer in VRAM, reserved from the bank's budget before the bank is filled; the grouping kernel looks file experts up; a miss is read from the file and written into the least recently used slot as the reader stages its rows (no extra bus bytes). (3) Shared expert as a reader pick (--fold-shared). (4) Option B (--batch-identical): R3 runs 1-4 tokens per job, a row's weights read once and each token's arithmetic the one-token path's (StreamJob::ntok); reader steps of 2-4 tokens put every R3-able product through it, so verify rows equal one-token rows bit for bit. (5) CUDA graph (--graph): the engine on its own stream (a null Stream now means the current one in gpu_ops / gpu_stream / gpu_prompt), the position read on the device (rope, attention with a fixed score stride and looping key blocks, K / V rows stored by kv_store), R3 counters cleared per run (run_reset); the layers of each kind of reader step (tokens, tail parity, verify, stamps) recorded the second time and replayed after; falls back to plain launches on a failed capture. (6) Prefetch (--prefetch): after each layer the next layer's router on this layer's output predicts its picks; a second stream copies the predicted file experts into cache slots; that layer's reader waits on an event. Tests: rung1 reader v3 = v24 bit for bit (F 256 half-warp / 1024 warp), cached fill-then-read bit for bit and served from VRAM, shared fold vs fp64; rung4 cache on / off, prefetch, prefetch + graph bit for bit; one-token vs verify-4 rows bit for bit with batch-identical, with and without the graph. Sandbox: -Werror clean, CUDA type check clean (gpu_ops, gpu_stream, gpu_prompt), unit ctest 5 / 5.
